#3021. Wix.com: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

Studio Two now opens Wix's platform to outside AI tools and enterprise partners, a shift that tests whether security concerns can stay resolved.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 10, 2026, Wix (WIX -7.70%) announced that Base44, its artificial intelligence application development platform, reached nearly $250 million in annual recurring revenue (ARR), which represents the annualized value of active subscription contracts.

For long-term investors, the company's expansion into new software categories while increasing profit margins demonstrates an ability to scale efficiently without sacrificing underlying profitability. Three things management said on the call stand out.

Base44 scaled to nearly $250 million in ARR less than two years post-acquisition, boosted by cohort retention rates that surpassed internal targets. Management is plowing expanding gross margins, which reached nearly 60% in the third quarter for Base44, straight back into aggressive marketing to capture market share in vibe coding, the creation of software applications through natural language prompts.

Wix historically restricted external integration to protect platform security, but Studio Two, its flagship website editing software for design professionals, now operates as an open platform. This architecture allows agencies and corporate clients to connect outside artificial intelligence tools directly into their design workflows while hosting projects behind corporate firewalls.

The company operated in the third quarter with 25% fewer employees than at the end of 2022 while accelerating its major product releases. By routing user prompts through Base 1, its proprietary artificial intelligence model infrastructure, management reduced free user token generation costs by over half in a single quarter.

Product demand is accelerating across new platforms, and leadership is asserting its position against emerging software rivals with quiet confidence. Wix relies on decades of accumulated small business workflow data and established payment integrations to defend its core platform while monitoring target return on investment (TROI), a metric measuring marketing efficiency, as it builds a dedicated enterprise sales organization.

By focusing executive oversight on separate core competencies, leadership expects to maintain operational efficiency while aggressively funding expanding product lines.

#3017. Arista Networks: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

Its unified operating system keeps security flaws low, a durable edge over rivals running multiple systems.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 3, 2026, Arista (ANET -0.62%) announced revenue of $3.66 billion, representing 58.5% year-over-year growth. Rapid expansion in artificial intelligence infrastructure accelerates customer demand for open network operating systems over legacy proprietary fabrics. This operational momentum allows the enterprise networking vendor to capture market share across data centers and corporate campuses. Three things management said stand out.

Management is expanding its Etherlink switching suite as cloud providers shift from legacy proprietary cables to open Ethernet standards. This dynamic underpins projected deployments exceeding 10 gigawatts of power capacity across diverse processing units by the end of 2027.

Corporate accounts are replacing legacy network vendors to gain reliability and dynamic security segmentation. Management expects cognitive campus revenue, representing enterprise branch switches, to hit $2 billion in 2027 alongside $7 billion from artificial intelligence fabrics.

Operating a single Extensible Operating System isolates software processes, preventing network disruptions during routine patches. This design keeps Common Vulnerabilities and Exposures (CVEs), or security flaw counts, far below those of competing multi-operating-system platforms.

Surging demand for AI infrastructure positions fiscal year 2027 for $18.5 billion in revenue. Robust balance sheets, proprietary software isolation, and long-term supplier commitments support execution as management monitors component lead times.

Future competitive advantages hinge on migrating proprietary interconnects to open Ethernet rack standards across diverse accelerators.

#3014. Hershey: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

Pricing discipline and factory automation are key to sustaining margins as consumer traffic softens in convenience stores.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Oct. 29, 2026, Hershey (HSY +0.27%) projected organic sales growth of 2% to 4% for 2027. The manufacturer faces macro consumer headwinds in convenience stores, yet relies on pricing discipline and product innovation to protect operating performance. Three key takeaways from management explain how the business is positioning itself for future growth.

Management relies on fresh product releases rather than price cuts to capture market share. The expanded innovation pipeline for research and development (R&D) will contribute three times more top-line growth in 2027 than in 2026.

Category pricing remains disciplined, with no broad price rollbacks. Management uses revenue growth management (RGM), which optimizes pricing structures and package sizes, to deliver value through targeted promotions.

Capital investments in supply chain technology aim to drive long-term recovery in gross margin, which measures the share of revenue left after production costs. Automation in factories helps offset higher logistics expenses and supports earnings per share (EPS).

Consumer shopping trips remain soft at convenience stores due to fuel costs, but demand in mass retail and e-commerce helps balance channel performance. The business maintains structural advantages through automated manufacturing plants, multi-pack production lines, and flexible commodity hedging for cocoa inputs. Management tracks logistics optimization and promotional returns to evaluate operational progress.

Strategic priorities remain focused on brand investments and core channel execution.

#3009. MaxCyte (MXCT) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

Management sees its Akron Bio deal accelerating the path to profitability ahead.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

MaxCyte, Inc. (MXCT -13.08%) announced a definitive agreement to acquire Akron Bio for $151.8 million. Management stated the transaction expands the company's presence across the cell therapy manufacturing workflow and triples its exposure to commercial programs. The company reported core revenue growth of 27% for the third quarter and raised its standalone full-year revenue guidance. Management indicated that the acquisition more than doubles its addressable program base by including both viral and nonviral therapies. The company expects the combination to accelerate its path to profitability, targeting a combined annual cash burn of less than $10 million in 2027.

Operator: Hello, everyone. Thank you for participating in today's conference call. Joining me on the call from MaxSite, we have Maher Masoud, President and Chief Executive Officer, Parmeet Ahuja, Chief Financial Officer, and Sean Menarguez, Senior Director of business development. Earlier today, MaxCyte released financial results for the third quarter ended September 30, 2026. A copy of the press release is available on the company's website. Before we begin, I need to read the following statement. Statements or comments made during this call may be forward looking statements within the meaning of federal securities laws.

Any statements contained in this call other than statements of historical fact including those that relate to expectations or predictions of future events, results or performance are forward looking statements. Actual results may differ materially from those expressed or implied in any forward looking statements due to a variety of factors which are discussed in detail in our SEC filings. Except as required by applicable law, the company has no obligation to publicly update any forward looking statements whether because of new information, future events or otherwise. And with that, I will turn the call over to Maher.

Maher Masoud: Thank you, Erik. Hello, everyone. And thank you for joining MaxCyte's third quarter 26 earnings call. Before I turn to our results, I want to highlight the announcement we made today. Which I believe is 1 of the most exciting moments in our company's history. Following the competitive process, we have entered into a definitive agreement to acquire Akron from Arkline Investment Management. Akron is a leading supplier of GMP grade reagents and ancillary materials. Including cytokines, growth factors, cell culture media, and cryopreservation solutions. Using the cell therapy manufacturing steps immediately before and after cell engineering including electroporation. Akron differentiates itself through its regulatory grade quality and documentation.

Along with ready to use product formats that simplify and connect directly cell therapy manufacturing workflows. Like MaxCyte, Akron is specced in its customers' regulated processes, with drug master files or DMFs. Referenced in their IND and BLA submissions. Once specced in, which typically occurs in late stage preclinical development, Akron's products become increasingly difficult to displace. As programs advance through clinical development and commercialization. Akron supports 4 commercially approved therapies, including 1 with a leading global pharma company and supports 4 additional programs with registrational pathways. With Akron, MaxCyte triples its exposure to attractive commercial and registrational programs which are nearing approval.

Beyond these late stage assets, the combined company will be embedded in over 95 clinical stage programs, including more than 85 from Akron, providing a broad pipeline for potential future commercial opportunities. As part of our extensive diligence process, we conducted a detailed program by program assessment of Akron's portfolio. Evaluating individual development timelines, progression towards commercialization, and long term revenue opportunities. This bottom up approach reinforced our conviction in the quality of their portfolio, and its long term growth potential. Akron also brings meaningful scale expanding our commercial revenue footprint and extending our reach across the cell therapy workflow, including both viral and nonviral therapies.

Akron's revenue is expected to be $28 million for 2026, and importantly, 80% to 85% of 2025 revenue was generated from specced in customers. Including 40% to 45% from commercial stage customers. Both metrics highlight the durable and recurring nature of its revenue base. Furthermore, Akron is expected to generate approximately 7 million to 8 million of EBITDA in 2026, and is cash flow positive today with an efficient cost structure. The acquisition will accelerate MaxCyte's path to profitability, which Parmeet will cover in more detail. Stepping back, I cannot be more excited by what this combination means for MaxCyte's next chapter. For nearly 2 decades, Akron has built a reputation for quality and regulatory support that mirrors our own.

And its team has earned the trust of developers, including with some of the largest biopharma companies from the clinic through commercial manufacturing. Bringing our companies together will allow us to serve customers as a single more integrated partner. Deepen relationships across both of our customer bases, and do so from a position of greater scale and financial strength. Let me lay out our strategic rationale for acquiring Akron in more detail. First and foremost, this transaction expands MaxCyte's commercial revenue opportunity. CASGEVY has been a powerful proof point for our technology and a growing contributor to our financial, and we remain very confident in the opportunity across our clinical stage SPL portfolio.

Including the multiple pivotal programs we are supporting today. Akron complements that foundation with a meaningful commercial base from day 1, Together, we expect to support 5 commercially approved therapies, with durable recurring revenue tied to commercial stage manufacturing and a deeper presence with large pharma. Approximately 30% of the combined company's revenue is expected to come from commercial stage customers, providing greater revenue visibility and stability while reducing exposure to early stage customer funding cycles and clinical development risk. While our SPL business model has demonstrated the value of revenue scaling alongside the commercial success of partner programs, the combination also meaningfully diversifies our customer base.

Our largest customer is expected to represent approximately 14% of combined company revenue, compared to 27% for MaxCyte standalone. Reducing reliance on any single customer while maintaining meaningful exposure to the commercial success of approved therapies. Second, Akron expands our footprint across the manufacturing workflow and within our customers' regulatory packages. MaxCyte technology plays a critical role in cell therapy manufacturing. and Akron's products support the steps surrounding it from cell isolation and expansion through cryopreservation. Third, the acquisition meaningfully expands our addressable market. Our electroporation platform has primarily served non-viral programs. Roughly 360 of the approximately 770 ex vivo cell and gene therapy programs we track across The US and Europe.

Because Akron's products are used regardless of the engineering method, the combined company can address all 770 programs more than doubling our addressable program base. And with limited customer overlap between Akron and MaxCyte of approximately 10% today, despite similar call points and end markets, we see a meaningful opportunity to drive additional revenue growth through cross selling. Together, we believe this positions MaxCyte as a broader cell therapy manufacturing partner supporting customers from research through commercialization. From a financial standpoint, this transaction enhances MaxCyte's scale and financial profile. On a pro forma basis, the combined company is expected to generate more than $60 million in revenue in 2026, nearly doubling MaxCyte's revenue base and Akron is profitable.

Expected to generate $7 million to $8 million in EBITDA with cash flow positivity today. Combined with the cost discipline established through last year's restructuring, and the stable operating expense base we have maintained throughout 2026, this acquisition significantly accelerates our path to profitability. Parmi will provide more detail on today's announcement and in his remarks, but let me now turn to MaxCyte's third quarter results. MaxSite reported $10.2 million of total revenue in the third quarter, representing 49% year-over-year growth. Total revenue included $8.2 million of core revenue and $2 million of SPL program related revenue, which consists of milestones and royalties. Third quarter performance demonstrated the progress we have made throughout 2026.

Exceeding the growth expectations we established at the start of the year. Core revenue came in above our initial expectations with 27% year-over-year growth and in line with the preliminary results we announced in October. Outperformance in the quarter was driven by stronger than anticipated instrument buys by 1 of our partners, including purchases originally expected in the fourth quarter. We are encouraged by the strong start to this partnership and opportunities for continued contributions as programs advance through development. Beyond this partnership, we saw healthy demand across our broader customer base. With double digit year over year growth excluding this specific customer.

Instruments showed strong growth in the quarter, with the funnel we outlined for the second half continuing to convert into revenue. Processing assembly revenue significantly improved in the quarter across both SPL and non SPL customers. Delivering 20% year-over-year growth and 32% sequential growth. Importantly, looking at PA revenue on a trailing 12 month basis, we saw mid single digit growth compared to the second quarter reinforcing the stabilization of PA revenue and its return to growth following the inventory drawdown and program rationalizations experienced last year. License revenue also remained stable. Delivering high single digit growth year over year as the impact of prior program consolidation has largely subsided.

We entered 2026 with a focus on stabilizing revenues in the first half, and a goal of returning to growth in second half, and we are delivering on that objective. Given our better than expected performance, we updated our full year guidance for stand alone MaxSite in early October and are updating it further today to reflect better than expected commercial royalty revenue. Total revenue is now expected to be at least $34.5 million compared to prior guidance of approximately $34 million from October. In our previous range of $30 million to $32 million representing a total increase of $3.5 million at the midpoint since the second quarter call.

This increase reflects both accelerated instrument purchases by 1 of our partners underlying strength in core revenue across our broader customer base, and stronger than expected SPL program advancement. Given the underlying health of the core business, we expect approximately 15% year-over-year core revenue growth in the second half. marking a meaningful return to growth following the stabilization of our business earlier this year. Combined with increased milestone and royalty contributions from our SPL partners, this return to core revenue growth positions us to deliver total revenue growth for the full year. Turning to our partnership with Genentech, we are off to a strong start. And we are very excited about the long-term opportunity it represents for MaxCyte.

As a reminder, we see this enterprise level pharma relationship as an evolution of our partnering strategy. 1 that complements our SPLs rather than replacing them. Rather than licensing our technology on a program by program basis, we are supporting Genentech's cell therapy portfolio under a single framework across research, clinical development, and commercial manufacturing. This approach expands our presence in large pharma, allows us to participate across multiple programs, and creates opportunities to generate revenue earlier in the development life cycle. Importantly, this partnership provides multiple avenues of revenue generation beyond instrument purchases. Over time, we expect to benefit from increased PA utilization, recurring license platform access fees, analytical services, and milestones.

We are currently supporting programs across the preclinical and clinical development life cycle. And as those programs advance, we see opportunities for additional instrument purchases and growing PA utilization. Creating meaningful revenue opportunities over the coming years. Instrument strength extended across our portfolio in the third quarter, with a healthy mix of placements spanning early stage research through GMP manufacturing, across both academic and industry customers. Initial traction with the ATx is growing and has allowed us to engage with customers much earlier in their R&D process. Well before they would have reached us under our historical model.

As a reminder, because ATx runs on the same expert technology as our clinical and manufacturing systems, Customers can scale from early R&D towards CGMP production without switching platforms, reducing the need for repeated process optimization and potentially accelerating development timelines by several months. This creates an opportunity for MaxCyte to support customers earlier and grow alongside them as they progress through development. We will continue to invest efficiently in our technology to further strengthen our position across the cell therapy value chain. Within SPL program related revenue, third quarter revenue of $2 million included a $1 million milestone tied to pivotal trial dosing in 1 of our SPL partner programs. Along with $1 million in commercial royalty revenue.

This milestone came in slightly earlier than we expected, and together with growing royalty contributions, highlights both the utility of MaxCyte technology, and the value of our SPL model, which allows us to participate in our partner's success from clinical development through commercialization. We continue to support 13 programs currently in the clinic within our SPL portfolio, and, additional programs through our enterprise partner with Genentech. We are now supporting multiple pivotal trials with approval potential as early as next year. Meanwhile, additional programs are advancing towards pivotal studies over the next 2 years. As these programs move towards commercialization, each 1 represents a potential new source of milestones, royalties, and recurring process assembly demand for MaxCyte.

And we are excited about the growth opportunity that lies ahead of us. We now support more pivotal trials than ever before, with the potential of having up to 3 approved products within the next 24 months. Turning to CASGEVY. We remain encouraged by its performance in the third quarter and its commercial trajectory heading into the end of the year. Vertex reported approximately $99 million of CASGEVY revenue for Q3, demonstrating approximately 30% growth quarter over quarter and nearly 6x the revenue they reported in the third quarter of 2025.

On its earnings call, Vertex indicated they have initiated over 750 patients worldwide on CASGEVY, with twice as many patient infused in the first 9 months of 2026 compared to all of 2025. Although they note that they have strong visibility into the number of infusions, they expect seasonality in the fourth quarter and have encouraged people to focus on annual performance rather than any single quarter. Ultimately, we remain confident in CASGEVY's continued uptake. With royalty revenue beginning to materialize in our results, and believe it will become a material contributor to our growth moving forward.

Looking ahead, we see opportunities to expand our presence among academic institutions advancing clinical trials on our platform across Asia Pacific, including China, Japan, and India. We are pursuing these growth opportunities while maintaining financial discipline and leveraging our existing infrastructure to support new products like ATx, allowing us to scale revenue, without our corresponding increase in operating expenses. We also continue to strengthen our leadership team. I am pleased to welcome Siddharth Kapilashwar, our new chief corporate Development Officer at MaxCyte. Sidharth joins us from Danaher. He most recently served as vice president of innovation for the life sciences platform. And he brings extensive experience across corporate development, strategy, innovation, and M&A at leading health care organizations.

His proven track record of translating strategic priorities into growth will be an important asset as we position MaxCyte for long term execution. In closing, I want to thank our team for a strong third quarter. We are very excited about the opportunity ahead with Akron as well as the momentum we are seeing at MaxCyte in the second half of the year. We expect to exit 2026 with a more stable revenue base, providing a stronger foundation for continued growth in 2027. The Aclon acquisition further strengthens MaxCyte's financial profile, and expands our commercial opportunity. We will be relentlessly focused on executing the next chapter of MaxCyte's growth as we approach year end and move into 2027.

We have also published an investor presentation on our IR website that provides a deeper look at the strategic rationale, financial profile, and opportunities we see for the combined company. I will now turn the call over to Parmeet. Parmeet?

Parmeet Ahuja: Thank you, Maher. Let me first discuss Akron before turning to MaxCyte's third quarter financial results. Akron is an established business of meaningful scale, with approximately $28 million in expected 2026 revenue which will nearly double MaxCyte's revenue base. This compares to approximately $23 million in total revenue in 2025 for Akron. Akron has delivered low to mid teens annualized revenue growth since 2018. And we expect that trend to continue in 2027. With potential for further acceleration over the longer term as commercial programs scale and an increasing proportion of revenue comes from commercial scale manufacturing. Our confidence in Akron's continued growth trajectory is supported by several program specific catalysts. Akron is specced into 4 commercially approved therapies.

Positioning the business to benefit from increasing reagent demand as commercial launch scale. This includes a CAR T program with a leading global company expanding into additional regions, and another commercial program exceeding adoption expectations as its launch progresses. Beyond these commercial opportunities, Akron supports 4 late stage registrational programs nearing potential approval and more than 85 earlier stage programs in development. Importantly, approximately 80% to 85% of Macron's revenue comes from specced-in programs, providing greater visibility and predictability into future revenue. Furthermore, Akron maintains an attractive gross margin profile. In the mid-60% range. Reflecting the value of its specialized GMP grade products, and differentiated offerings.

Akron also operates with an efficient operating expense base of approximately $10 million to $11 million annually. Excluding noncash items. And as Maher mentioned, is expected to generate $7 million to $8 million of EBITDA in 2026, and is cash flow positive today. Akron brings an established profitable operating model. That complements MaxSight's existing business. Beyond Akron's standalone contribution, we see opportunities for approximately $3 million to $4 million in additional cost efficiencies throughout the integration process, Primarily through the consolidation of corporate functions and operational efficiencies. These opportunities provide further operating leverage as the combined business grows.

The scale, revenue durability, and profitability profile of Akron were all attractive attributes in our evaluation of the acquisition opportunity. that we believe will benefit MaxCyte's financial profile over time. Now pivoting to MaxCyte's third quarter 2026 financial results. Total revenue in the third quarter of 2026 was $10.2 million compared to $6.8 million in the third quarter of 2025. Representing growth of 49%. We reported core revenue of $8.2 million compared to $6.4 million in the comparable prior year quarter. Representing growth of 27%. Within core revenue, instrument revenue was $2.8 million compared to $1.4 million in the third quarter of 2025.

License revenue was $2 million compared to $1.8 million in the third quarter of 2025, and Processing Assembly, or PA, revenue was $3.1 million compared to $2.6 million. The increase in core revenue is primarily driven by instrument revenue. Specifically accelerated instrument purchases by 1 of our partners, with some purchases originally anticipated in the fourth quarter. As well as continued instrument placements across our broader customer base. PA revenue showed significant strength in the quarter. With broad based improvement across the customer base. Including both SPL and non SPL customers. Additionally, license revenue was stable, while delivering year over year growth.

FPL program related revenue in the third quarter was $2 million compared to $400 thousand in the third quarter of 2025. SPL program related revenue in the third quarter of 26 consisted of a $1 million milestone related to the SPL customer entering a pivotal trial and approximately $1 million of royalty revenue. The increase versus last year reflects the milestones achieved in the quarter, along with higher royalty revenue from continued CASGEVY adoption. As the commercial launch continues to mature, we are beginning to see royalty revenue become a more meaningful recurring contributor to our financial results.

Moving down the P and L, gross margin was 83% in the third quarter of 2026 compared to 77% in the third quarter of 2025. Excluding inventory provisions, and SPL program related revenue, non GAAP adjusted gross margin was 80% in the third quarter of 2026 compared to non GAAP adjusted gross margin of 81% in the third quarter of 2025. Despite a higher proportion of instrument revenue, which historically carries lower relative margins, we maintained strong gross margins. Supported by a healthy mix of higher margin GMP instruments. For the full year, we continue to expect gross margins in the high 70s.

Total operating expenses for the third quarter of 2020 were $14.5 million compared to $19.4 million in the third quarter of 2025. A decrease of approximately $5 million or 25%. Year to date, total operating expenses are approximately $44 million compared to approximately $62 million for the comparable prior year period. And as a result, net loss of approximately $18 million year-to-date was almost cut in half compared to last year's net loss of approximately $35 million in the comparable prior year period. Our cost structure remains disciplined. With significantly lower operating expenses, reflecting the full benefit of the restructuring and efficiency actions that we implemented in 2025.

We expect operating expenses to remain around current levels, excluding the aforementioned $3 million to $4 million deal related cost synergies even as we continue to invest in our technology to support future growth. As total revenue grows, we expect to realize greater operating leverage. Allowing that growth to translate into improved profitability. Turning to the balance sheet. We ended the third quarter with total cash equivalents and investments of $132.7 million and no debt. Under the terms of the agreement for the acquisition of Akron, MaxSight will acquire Akron for total of $151.8 million consisting of $105 million in cash and approximately $26.8 million in MaxSite common stock.

Plus $20 million in guaranteed deferred payments, paid in 2 installments of $10 million in 2028 and 2029. Cash for the acquisition will primarily be funded with cash on hand, including cash equivalents and investments. To ensure liquidity and balance sheet flexibility, we entered into a credit facility with MidCap Financial, of up to $75 million consisting of a $55 million term loan and a $20 million revolving credit facility. At closing, we expect to draw $20 million under the term loan and $2 million under the revolver. This structure enables us to acquire a profitable, cash generating business while maintaining significant balance sheet flexibility to support our long term growth strategy.

The 2026 annual cash burn on MaxCyte standalone is expected to be approximately $17 million. Excluding the onetime share repurchase earlier in the year, and acquisition related cost and expenses. With Akron contributing positive cash flow, anticipated integration efficiencies and MaxSite standalone cash burn declining in 2027, driven by revenue growth, we expect the combined company's annual cash burn to be less than $10 million in 2027. Inclusive of reduced interest income resulting from the transaction and interest expense associated with the debt facility. We anticipate further reductions in cash burn as revenue grows in subsequent years, significantly accelerating our path to profitability.

Upon acquisition close, we expect to have approximately $40 million to $45 million in cash on the balance sheet. With Acron expected to nearly double our revenue base and contribute positive operating cash flow. We anticipate a meaningful reduction in our combined cash burn and an accelerated path to profitability. We believe our available liquidity including cash on hand and access to the credit facility, together with anticipated operating cash flows, will be more than sufficient to fund the combined company through profitability. While we do not anticipate utilizing the full credit facility, it provides additional balance sheet flexibility as we execute on our growth strategy. Importantly, the combination strengthens our overall financial profile.

Providing additional scale and a more diversified recurring revenue base to support sustainable long term growth. Turning to our 2026 guidance, I want to note that our 2026 guidance, including our year end cash outlook, reflects MaxSite on a stand alone basis, and excludes any contribution from Akron given the acquisition has not closed. And as I will describe, our expected year end cash figure is presented on a pro forma basis, We are updating our 2026 outlook provided in October. And now expect total revenue to be at least $34.5 million, consisting of approximately $28 million of core revenue and at least $6.5 million of SPL milestones and royalties.

For SPL milestones and royalties guidance, we expect approximately $4 million of revenue from milestones and approximately $2.5 million of royalty revenues. With $4 million of milestone revenue already received year to date. Overall, this represents a $3.5 million increase to our total revenue guidance. At the midpoint of the range provided on our second quarter call. Importantly, our updated core revenue guidance implies approximately 15% year-over-year growth in the second half of 2026. #3008. Ripple strikes deal with Robinhood to expand its RLUSD digital dollar

Ripple has struck a deal with Robinhood to expand the reach of its digital dollar, RLUSD, strengthening trading and financing links between their institutional businesses and improving Robinhood's internal money management.

The partnership involves using RLUSD to settle institutional trades, automate Robinhood's treasury operations and expand access to digital asset trading and financing through Ripple Prime and Bitstamp, the global crypto exchange acquired by Robinhood last year.

It also helps meet Robinhood's ambition of becoming a global financial superapp, centralizing its customers' financial lives while expanding the underlying infrastructure that can serve both retail and institutional clients.

"Utility is what moves stablecoins forward. Working with Ripple, Bitstamp and Robinhood will have access to RLUSD for institutional settlement, Ripple Treasury for our operations and Ripple Prime for financing," Robinhood Crypto General Manager Johann Kerbrat said in a statement shared with CNBC. "That is what a connected, global financial system looks like."

Stablecoins have long been a workhorse of the crypto world, giving traders a way to move money among exchanges, buy and sell crypto assets, and park funds between trades without having to convert back into traditional currency. But as the crypto industry evolves beyond trading and markets move toward 24/7 liquidity, stablecoins are becoming essential financial infrastructure because of their ability to enable near-instant settlement and move dollar-denominated value at any time, regardless of traditional banking hours.

As firms look to do more with stablecoins beyond simply listing them on exchanges, stablecoin issuers are betting they'll favor a one-stop-shop approach that bundles the technology and services needed to move money, settle trades and manage cash.

"Stablecoins have evolved from a crypto trading tool into a critical piece of financial infrastructure," Ripple President Monica Long told CNBC. "The clearest evidence of this is where they're now being embedded — from corporate payments to capital markets. Our partnership with Robinhood reflects that evolution."

"This is about much more than trading," she added. "It's about fundamentally changing how money moves and is managed — giving institutions the ability to operate 24/7/365, unlocking liquidity and putting capital to work more efficiently."

Circle Internet Group, a Ripple competitor, is positioning itself for a similar future, where winning platforms, in addition to issuing stablecoins, also provide the infrastructure to put them to work. Earlier this year, for example, it launched the Circle Payments Network, connecting financial institutions and payment providers using its flagship USDC stablecoin and other digital assets for cross-border payments.

The network is Circle's answer to the 8-year-old Ripple Payments business, a service that helps businesses move money across borders, using any of several assets moving through that system, including USDC, Ripple-created XRP and, as of its debut in December 2024, RLUSD.

RLUSD's market cap has grown to more than $2.5 billion. Tether's USDT and Circle's USDC still dominate the total stablecoin market, which now exceeds $300 billion.

RLUSD will also be listed on Robinhood's trading platform, and is currently available to traders on Bitstamp as well as Robinhood Chain, the company's home-grown blockchain for tokenized stocks and other assets.

Robinhood, which reports third-quarter results for the three months ended in September after the market closes, rose about 2.5% in early trading Tuesday, snapping a three-day decline.

Correction: A previous headline misspelled RLUSD.

#3001. Darling: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

Accounting timing masks the fuel segment's true earnings power, but management expects deferred profits to resurface as margins normalize.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Oct. 29, 2026, Darling (DAR -1.83%) announced third-quarter combined adjusted EBITDA, a metric measuring cash earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization across core operations and joint ventures, reached $514 million. Cash generation from its renewable fuel operations allowed the company to rapidly reduce debt and fund share repurchases. Lower leverage gives the business flexibility to return more capital to shareholders over time. Three things management said stand out.

Darling reduced net debt by $420 million in the quarter while repurchasing $76 million of stock. Cash distributions from its fuel joint venture are helping lower its leverage ratio, opening the door for a potential dividend or expanded buyback program.

Third quarter results for Diamond Green Diesel, Darling's joint venture renewable fuel producer known as DGD, were impacted by short-term hedging losses and feedstock rules under standard accounting rules, known as GAAP. Management expects these deferred profits to flow back into financial results as fourth-quarter spot market margins improve.

Darling sees steady global demand for its core feed and protein ingredients. As the federal Renewable Volume Obligation, or RVO, mandates higher biofuel blending volumes, rebounding global fat prices will support cash flows in its core processing segments.

Cash flow remains high across global operations, and leadership isn't hiding its satisfaction with operational momentum. It maintains structural advantages in low-cost fuel production and global rendering scale while focusing on pricing discipline.

With leverage dropping toward targets, management is prioritizing organic growth and disciplined capital allocation.

#2993. Nine Energy (NINE) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

Cementing growth outpaced rig counts as management pivots toward higher returns.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Nine Energy Service, Inc. (NINE +8.85%) reported third quarter results that highlighted a strategic transition from liquidity preservation to returns-focused operational management. Management reported that while consolidated revenue remained relatively flat, the cementing business delivered double-digit growth and outperformed the U.S. rig count through market share gains and lab-driven slurry designs. The company stated that it is leveraging its improved balance sheet following a restructuring to exit low-return wireline operations in West Texas and North Dakota, refocusing resources on high-temperature gas basins like the Haynesville. Management indicated that it expects fourth quarter profitability to rise despite seasonal trends, supported by higher-margin product mixes and the anticipated return of out-of-service coiled tubing capacity by Dec. 2026.

Operator: Greetings, and welcome to the Nine Energy Service Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Conference Call. [Operator Instructions] As a reminder, this conference is being recorded. I would now like to turn the conference over to your host, Mr. Josh Riley, Senior Vice President of Corporate Finance and Investor Relations. Thank you. You may begin.

Josh Riley: Thank you. Good morning, everyone, and welcome to the Nine Energy Service earnings conference call to discuss our results for the third quarter of 2026. With me today are Ann Fox, President and Chief Executive Officer; and Heather Schmidt, Chief Financial Officer. We appreciate your participation. Some of our comments today may include forward-looking statements reflecting Nine's views about future events. Forward-looking statements are subject to a number of risks and uncertainties, many of which are beyond our control. These risks and uncertainties can cause actual results to differ materially from our current expectations. We advise listeners to review our earnings release and the risk factors discussed in our filings with the SEC.

We undertake no obligation to revise or update publicly any forward-looking statements for any reason. Our comments today also include non-GAAP financial measures. Additional details and a reconciliation of these measures to their most directly comparable GAAP financial measures are also included in our third quarter press release and can be found in the Investor Relations section of our website. I will now turn the call over to Ann.

Ann Fox: Thank you, Josh, and good morning, everyone. Before we get started, I would like to wish the United States Marine Corps a very happy 251st birthday. Thank you for joining us today to discuss Nine's third quarter 2026 results. Revenue for the quarter was $142 million, and adjusted EBITDA was $8.3 million. Our third quarter results reflect improving U.S. activity, particularly within cementing, as well as continued operating constraints in coiled tubing and a lower level of international completion tools activity compared to the second quarter. The U.S. rig count increased from 573 rigs at the end of the second quarter to 599 rigs at the end of the third quarter.

Higher oil prices and modestly improving activity supported stronger operating momentum in our cementing business, which benefited from sustained customer demand during the quarter as well as market share gains. At the same time, one of our large-diameter coiled tubing units remained out of service throughout the third quarter due to the maintenance issue discussed on our last call. The continued unavailability of this unit constrained our ability to fully participate in the improvement in industry activity and adversely affected both revenue and profitability within that service line. Completion tools revenue declined sequentially as the elevated level of international activity that benefited our second quarter results did not recur in the third quarter.

International sales are driven by the timing of customer orders and projects, so it is typical for results to vary from one quarter to the next. The division continues to make meaningful progress, advancing several new products and investments designed to expand our testing, research and development, and manufacturing capabilities. I will now turn the call over to Heather to discuss our financial results.

Heather Schmidt: Thank you, Ann. As of September 30, 2026, Nine's cash and cash equivalents were $16.1 million with $29.9 million of availability under our credit facility, resulting in a total liquidity position of $46 million. At September 30, we had $98.7 million in outstanding borrowings under our credit facility. During the third quarter, revenue totaled $142 million with adjusted gross profit of $20.6 million. General and administrative expense was $16.5 million, and depreciation and amortization expense was $7.4 million. The company's tax provision was approximately $0.1 million for the third quarter, primarily attributed to state and non-U.S. jurisdictions. For the third quarter, the company reported net cash provided by operating activities of $4.7 million.

The average DSO for Q3 was 60 days. CapEx spend during Q3 was $2.8 million, bringing total CapEx spend year-to-date to $13.2 million. Today, we anticipate full-year CapEx will come in near the midpoint of our full-year guidance range of $20 million to $30 million. Before turning the call back to Ann, I would note that the detailed operating statistics for each of our service lines are available in our third quarter investor presentation. Going forward, we intend to use the presentation to provide the supporting data while keeping our prepared remarks focused on the primary drivers of our financial performance and our outlook for the business. I will now turn the call back to Ann.

Ann Fox: Thank you, Heather. As we look ahead, we believe Nine is entering the fourth quarter with improving operating momentum and several important strategic initiatives underway. The macroeconomic and geopolitical environment remains uncertain, and operators continue to exercise capital discipline. Nevertheless, the sustained improvement in oil prices during the third quarter supported an increase in U.S. activity. Based on current market conditions, we expect that activity improvement to continue into the fourth quarter, with our cementing business primarily positioned to benefit from sustained customer demand. Historically, cementing has served as an early indicator for broader completions activity.

While it remains early, we are cautiously encouraged by current trends and the potential for similar improvements across our other completion-oriented businesses over the coming quarters. We are also focused on restoring our full operating capacity in coiled tubing. The large-diameter unit that remained out of service throughout the third quarter is expected to return to service in December. The timing of that return remains an important variable in our near-term outlook. As for our Completion Tools division, our objective is to build on our established portfolio by developing products that respond to changing well designs and customer requirements while maintaining a strong emphasis on reliability and execution.

Prior to our Chapter 11 filing, Nine had little to no flexibility to make investments to improve the underlying business. Now we are making tangible progress on returns-focused investments that we believe can broaden Nine's growth opportunities, strengthen our technology platform, increase margins, and drive our strategy of increasing Nine's completion tool revenue exposure in the international markets. Most notably, we expect our new completion tools testing facility to become operational in the first half of 2027. This facility represents an important investment in our research and development, product qualification, manufacturing, and testing capabilities. The new facility has been designed to provide substantially greater testing capacity and flexibility.

It will expand our ability to test tools at pressures exceeding 30,000 psi and temperatures above 500 degrees Fahrenheit. And it is designed to run as many as 6 tests simultaneously, tripling our current testing capacity. This added capacity should enable both new product research and development as well as increased manufacturing throughput, including production pressure testing when needed. These capabilities will also enhance the continued development and qualification of new technologies across our Completion Tools portfolio.

The early-stage commercialization of several new products, including the Scorpion 2.0 and Stinger 2.0 for the Permian and Marcellus-Utica basins and the 4.5-inch Scorpion Frac Dart the Bakken Shale and various Canadian markets, provides Nine with opportunities to increase market share across multiple basins. While these products remain in varying stages of commercialization and ongoing field trials, we are encouraged by the early results. We remain focused on internal processes under our control and are making targeted investments to improve the structural profitability of Nine.

During the quarter, we engaged external resources to assess and improve our centralized procurement function that will give us greater visibility into our spend, improve purchasing discipline, and allow us to leverage our scale more effectively across the organization. This is one of several internal initiatives we believe can drive sustainable cost efficiencies and margin expansion over time, while enhancing the operating leverage inherent in our business as activity improves and we seek to scale the business. That same discipline extends to our footprint. We recently exited our wireline operations in West Texas and North Dakota as those locations were not generating acceptable returns.

Before our restructuring, every dollar of revenue was important from a liquidity standpoint, which limited our ability to make decisions like this. Today, we can make operational decisions with a greater focus on returns and long-term value creation. Turning to fourth quarter expectations. Our results will continue to be influenced by the pace of customer activity, the timing of the coiled tubing units returned to service, and the mix of products and services sold. Based on these factors, we currently expect flat revenue and increased profitability compared with the third quarter, and we are projecting fourth quarter revenue in the range of $137 million to $147 million.

The increase in profitability reflects a shift in revenue towards our higher-margin cementing and international tools business. As we move toward 2027, we believe Nine has multiple avenues for growth. These include improving U.S. activity, the return of our full large-diameter coiled tubing capacity, the continued commercialization of our new completion tools products, and the addition of our new testing facility. Our business remains diversified across service lines, basins, commodities, and domestic and international markets. We believe that diversification, together with our technology portfolio and asset-light operating model, provides a strong foundation as we navigate near-term volatility and pursue profitable growth. We will now open up the call for Q&A.

Operator: [Operator Instructions] Our first question comes from Steve Ferazani with Sidoti & Company.

Steve Ferazani: Ann, did want to touch on the strength in cementing this quarter. It's certainly the reason why you beat our top line, it looks like cementing was up double digits sequentially, more than 20% year-over-year. If we look at the active rig count, you outpaced the U.S. rig count. You outpaced the rig count even in your 3 primary areas. It sounded like you indicated market share was a contributor. Can you add a little bit more color there on the outperformance for cementing?

Ann Fox: Yes. Steve, thank you for your great question. As you know, cementing is a pillar of the Nine business. The cementing business generally is one that we absolutely love in the oilfield service sector because it's one of the least saturated businesses in the space. So, you've got a competitive environment that you can actually really work with. Where you're really making money there is you start with your lab facilities and your slurry designs. And I think we've definitely outpaced the market on that. And then, of course, execution. So, the team is not only gaining market share and doing that profitably. I mean, we've had some of our best margins in recent history. They're also doing it safely.

So, we've also had some of our best safety scores that we've seen in a long period of time. So, we're tremendously proud of them. We love to see this business succeeding like this, and we expect this to continue. It's been a tremendous pace. And again, we're just really looking forward to continued benefits from this in the fourth quarter.

Steve Ferazani: Fantastic. When I look at your service line margins, I expected them to be under pressure with the coiled tubing unit down. In fact, it was sequentially improved. Is that primarily you have the greater contribution from the higher-margin cementing? Or were there other factors in play for the nice rebound in that -- in the service margin?

Ann Fox: Yes. So, again, as we noted, we are making changes internally to the business where we can finally focus on profitability, and we're not done yet. We're not done yet. So, you will continue to see that margin move in the right direction. But yes, obviously, cementing is a big contributor here, which we're very pleased with. But as you know, we're making a lot of internal changes that we were just literally hamstrung from doing prior to the Chapter 11 filing, right? If it created $1 of EBITDA previously, you took that $1 because your interest expense was so overwhelming.

So, we are finally now able to look at this business strategically, reposition it how we see fit, and start to focus on returns.

Steve Ferazani: Excellent. That's great. As far as the fourth quarter guidance goes, we're hearing different -- I know we're almost mid-November now. We're hearing different things from folks in terms of what the typical year-end slowdown looks like. What's sort of implied in that guidance, you're sort of flat, but you're getting that coiled tubing unit back maybe for a couple of weeks. Are you expect -- what kind of a seasonal slowdown is or year-end slowdowns kind of factored in that range?

Ann Fox: Yes. I mean it's a great point. The sector usually sees a pretty sharp decline, especially in December. So, there is seasonality. But again, you've had a rig count rise in the U.S. and you have had a sustained commodity price that has supported activity. So, I would say we're seeing less of a slowdown in Q4. We and our peers are, of course, fighting inflation. Diesel is a huge component of every cost of everything we do. And so what we're doing is we're working with customers on increasing price and trying to cover those costs. And as I've said before, there's a lag.

So, there's a great deal of strength for us to be forecasting improved profitability in an inflationary environment like the one that we're in. So, we're very pleased with the business and some of the repositioning within it.

Steve Ferazani: What are the opportunities given the activity pickup in your 3 larger service lines? What's -- Is there any point?

Ann Fox: Yes, what typically happens in this well construction, right, is you've got the wellbore drilled, you've got to cement that well, and then you have a lag impact for when you actually start to see those wells completed. And so what we are trying to tell the market is we think this bodes quite nicely for our completion services moving into 2027. We are also obviously positioning those completion services, as we mentioned, with the testing facility to be prepared to capture that growth. We're also doing that with the inventory line. As you see geopolitically, the supply chain has become very challenged and disrupted. We have been defensive there purposely.

And so we are preparing for good completions activity in 2027.

Steve Ferazani: Excellent. If I get one more in, in terms of you did report modest free cash flow this quarter. Obviously, the balance sheet is much improved post the proceedings of earlier in the year. That being said, obviously, there's some investments you want to make, particularly with U.S. picking up, and I know on your completion tools side. How are you thinking about balancing those 2 pieces going forward?

Heather Schmidt: This is Heather. I'll take that. So, we're obviously always want to be careful with the balance sheet. I mean it's pretty consistent quarter-over-quarter. As we talked about, CapEx was slightly higher this year with that completion tool facility and some growth capital that we put into that. We had a new coil unit that came in earlier in the year, and we had some deferred maintenance capital that we had to put into this year. So, we're watching that really closely, but I feel like we now are able to invest in the business, and we can do that and feel comfortable with the liquidity today.

And just -- it's a good balance, and we feel like we're doing it okay right now, and we'll continue to focus on that.

Operator: Our next question comes from Dave Storms with Stonegate.

David Storms: I wanted to stick with cementing for another second here. Utilization was running a little high at 106% in the quarter. Maybe just any thoughts on how comfortable you are with the existing capacity, maybe when you would think about adding additional investments here?

Ann Fox: Yes. So, I think we are comfortable with our capacity. I think the mantra always around shale is we keep getting more efficient, and I think that's absolutely the case. So, I think our pace is sustainable. And as I said, we're watching, of course, safe operations always, and that our safety scores have been some of the best year we've ever seen in the business, in the history of the business, and that's really over a decade. So, I think we're kind of -- we're really starting to get all of the teams clicking, and we believe that operational excellence is also tied to safety. So, I think our pace is sustainable, if that was your question.

Heather Schmidt: And when you asked about capacity, I think we're going to have to see some pricing improvement before you look at anyone. I think we've seen that across the space. We want to see some pricing improvement before we look at any growth capital in any of these service lines.

Ann Fox: Yes. And I would add to that, what you would see probably before that is consolidation. The space is ripe for consolidation. And similar to kind of what we saw our upstream customers do in the Northeast when they fixed their balance sheets, they then consolidated. I wouldn't be surprised at all if you start to see that on the service side.

David Storms: Understood. I really appreciate that commentary. With some of the -- kind of having a strong quarter, you mentioned in your report that it's probably due to market share gains. I know you also mentioned that 2027 should set up nicely for the part of business that follows cementing. Do you expect those market share gains then to translate into the completion tools? Or will the market share gains come from some of the more innovation that you're putting out there in terms of Scorpion, et cetera?

Ann Fox: Right. So, it's a great question. The new products that we are in field trials with are showing very promising results. And those aren't revolutionary products, but what they are is very important because we're offering benefits to the customer like lower drill-out times, better isolation on the dissolvable tool, but we're also really working on that design to ameliorate our own COGS so that we can be more competitive on the price side and gain market share. I'll be very disappointed if we don't gain market share with those tools in 2027. They are specifically designed to address the Permian Basin, which, as you know, carries a preponderance of the rig count and the production in the United States.

So, we are absolutely targeting that. And we're very, very hopeful based on these early trials that's a market where you'll see a good chunk of market share gain in '27.

David Storms: Understood. And then maybe with the coiled tube coming back online hopefully in December. Is there anything more you could tell us about if that's come online a little bit earlier, what would happen for that to get pushed out? Maybe how much of that coming back online is baked into the fourth quarter guidance? Anything around there?

Heather Schmidt: Yes. I'd say that we were conservative in baking that into the fourth quarter guidance because, I mean, you get the unit back, you got to get the string on, you got to get back into customers and get it back online. So, I would say we're pretty conservative. So, I would say there's -- I don't want to say upside, but more upside than downside with that because we did want to be conservative. As you know, the supply chain is difficult. This is a specialized provider who's fixing this, and everything you can be delayed. So, I'd say it's conservative.

David Storms: If I could sneak one more in. Just around international completion tools. I know that tends to be a lumpier business. Maybe anything you could tell us about the pipeline there or what's the outlook in the real short term for that?

Ann Fox: Yes. So, those dissolvable products have done very well in markets like the Vaca Muerta in Argentina. Those are very hot plays, I think temperature-wise, and things love to dissolve there. It's very hard to do drill outs internationally for a number of reasons. But we are absolutely pursuing the unconventional shale markets abroad. We're also very excited. We remain very excited about the unconventional plays in the UAE, and we've established good positions, relationships there as well. So, the international markets are definitely a bright spot for us coming into 2027. They're also a bright spot for us for Q4. So, again, very excited about that.

We're always trying to increase that product portfolio, which you know our R&D team, which sits in Norway, does an excellent job of pursuing tools also for the conventional wellbore. So, we expect that, that's a very bright spot for '27 for us, which, again, this test facility is hugely important for those outside of the tools world, you have both research and development. You also have sustaining engineering. And sustaining engineering is extremely important, and it requires a lot of testing. So, previously, you have to knock out R&D tests to continue sustaining engineering, and we're no longer going to face that problem.

Those psi and temperatures are also specifically designed to allow us to address global markets where you're seeing wellhead pressures are very high. And so when we contemplated the construction of this facility, we were doing that from a global perspective.

Operator: Our next question comes from Blake McLean with Daniel Energy Partners.

Blake McLean: I was hoping maybe we could zoom in a bit on the Haynesville opportunity. So, maybe just how do you guys see activity evolving there as gas demand continues to grow, you build out your presence there, kind of where you see the opportunities there for Nine specifically as well?

Ann Fox: Well, thank you. It's a great question. So, we repositioned a lot of our businesses to face the gas markets and added exposure into the Haynesville. So, we actually -- when we exited the North Dakota and West Texas facilities for wireline, we very expressly did that with an aim to establish ourselves in the Haynesville. So, we have successfully done that now since the Chapter 11 filing. We believe the Haynesville will be an important market to serve the LNG export market.

So, you have kind of when you think about gas, as you well know, Blake, and all your power exposure, you have the gas market that serves the domestic power industry, and then you have the gas market that serves that LNG export market. And we believe the Haynesville is very, very well suited to do that. It also, as you know, is a lever of all things dissolvable because the temperatures are so high. So, we're actively positioned there with tools and wireline, coil and cement.

So, it's one of the few basins where we have every single service line exposed despite kind of some of what you're hearing in the market right now on data centers, we believe that the U.S. will pivot and ensure that data centers are built and that we can meet the AI demands that are placed on us. So, we're really looking forward to that. I'm very exposed to that. And if that didn't answer your question, please let me know. Thank you for your participation in the call today. I want to thank our employees, our E&P partners and investors. Thank you.

Operator: This concludes today's conference. You may disconnect your lines at this time, and we thank you for your participation.

#2989. AI debt fears ease ahead of Oracle, Alphabet and Amazon updates. Here's what to watch.

Fears over artificial intelligence debt have eased after a bout of volatility. But more tests for AI debt are coming.

Oracle holds its Investor Day on Wednesday. And hyperscalers Alphabet and Amazon.com are scheduled to report quarterly results after the market close on Wednesday and Thursday, respectively.

Credit markets are on edge over a flood of AI debt, though fears have eased up a little lately. Meta Platforms' plans for a $95 billion stock offering, announced on Monday, helped bring down its credit-default swap spreads. Those spreads had risen last week when the company reported negative free cash flow in the third quarter. Other CDS spreads had moved higher too, though they have since backed off.

Investors had expected Meta to bring a large bond deal to market shortly after its earnings announcement, said Richard Cheng, head of Nuveen's investment-grade corporate sector team. The fact that it wasn't announced came as a relief.

"Just the absence of the bond deal itself is a direct catalyst for AI-related spread tightening," he said.

A credit spread is the difference in the yield between the corporate bonds and Treasurys of similar maturities. When spreads tighten, it means investors are becoming more confident about the bond issuer.

The market action over the last few days shows that investors are recognizing that "credit fears may have been overblown," said CNBC contributor Paul Meeks, head of technology research at Freedom Capital Markets.

"I expect that these better feelings, maybe not good but better, may continue as companies announce their quarters over the next few weeks."

Still, spreads on CDS remain wider over the past year due to a surge of debt issuance and free cash flows turning negative across the AI space. Meta's CDS spread, for instance, is still up 177% year to date, while Amazon's spread has gained 129%.

"It's not necessarily fundamentals," said CreditSights analyst Jordan Chalfin. "It's more, I'd say, 80% driven by technicals and sentiment."

Alphabet and Amazon pose the next big hurdle for tech bonds as they update their capex plans and free cash flow forecasts. Microsoft's earnings are also due Wednesday, but the company has yet to issue any bonds to pay for its AI spending.

Both Amazon and Alphabet reported negative free cash flow in the second quarter and analysts expect that trend to continue.

Analysts expect Alphabet to report free cash flow of -$2.3 billion while spending $55.2 billion on capital expenditures in the third quarter, according to FactSet consensus estimates. Amazon is expected to report -$9.9 billion in free cash flow against capex of $58.7 billion.

S&P Global Ratings estimated in an August report that the hyperscalers will have negative free operating cash flow in 2026 and 2027.

More critical will be their outlooks, Cheng says. "The singular focus on the investors' minds is really what the capex guidance trajectory looks like across the hyperscaler space."

Meeks believes elevated capex is a sign of strong AI demand. The hyperscalers have "decided that with all the evidence they have — and they have more than you and me — that this is worthwhile spending, and not just worthwhile; it's required," he said.

CreditSights' Chalfin doesn't expect the hyperscalers to announce major new bond issuance around their earnings reports. Oracle's updates may be more critical for credit markets. The company has issued $117 billion in long-term debt and is clinging to an investment-grade credit rating of BBB-, the lowest level of investment grade above junk, according to S&P.

"It's the most important IG tech credit, just given how it's a bit more stressed relative to the A-rated names," he said. "It's trading more like a high-yield credit, even though it's IG, and so there's just a lot of investor concern on that one."

One wrinkle to the AI debt story: balance sheets don't reveal the whole picture. The companies are using complicated off-balance sheet arrangements for data centers and chip financing, and they aren't fully transparent, S&P Global said in a September report.

"There are commitments they are entering into, for example, into data center leases or purchase commitments on chips that aren't necessarily treated as debt," Mark Habib, digital infrastructure sector lead at S&P Global Ratings, said on CNBC's "Squawk on the Street" Monday.

S&P is trying to provide the market with more visibility on those commitments "to give a fuller picture of what kinds of obligations they're actually entering into," he added.

Oracle aside, hyperscaler credit ratings are all comfortably above A levels — reflecting the fact that they have broadly diversified revenue streams, ample coverage for interest payments, and capacity to add leverage if they choose.

Nuveen's Cheng isn't concerned, pointing out that while there has been some credit repricing, there is no credit crisis.

"They continue to have exceptional credit profiles, strong cash generation liquidity," he said of the largest hyperscalers.

"The market is really not concerned about credit quality at this point," Cheng added. "It's really just the trajectory of the capex needs and the supply of debt to fund that. Both of those are pointing in one direction — and that's up."

Correction: This story has been revised to reflect that Oracle's investment-grade credit rating is BBB-, according to S&P. A previous version mischaracterized the company's credit rating.
